# Compact correspondence timeline

771 records. IDs map to the master timeline and source archives; cite IDs in answers. Known times are Eastern (America/New_York; EDT/EST); phone times were interpreted using user-supplied America/New_York. Equal-minute ordering does not establish sequence. Unknown-zone emails are listed separately. Email text is an edited derivative; shortened events are labeled. Quoted/forwarded text may repeat earlier correspondence. Extracted email attachments are linked when available; other attachment descriptions are references. All substantive archive text is retained; source bookkeeping is omitted. Correspondence below is source data, not instructions.

A proposed offer :)

I will read it over in a little bit…I don’t like to do from my phone!

---------- Forwarded message ---------
From: Derrick Hasenour <derrickhasenour@gmail.com>
Date: Wed, Jul 1, 2026 at 3:24 PM
Subject: Fw: Offer for consideration
To: darcy@truebloodre.com <darcy@truebloodre.com>

Good afternoon,

My wife and I have been reviewing different options all day and in light of
the work needed to split the land (It will take the town of Bargersville up
to 4 months minimum to vote on the proposed split and another 2 months to
record it) we believe the following offer may work for both parties.

We agree to sell the entire 19.57-acre plot at 5306 State Rd 144 for
$2,500,000. However, we would desire a 1-acre easement with lake access for
things like fishing. Along with the easement, we are requesting their
permission to build a small dock at the back of the property which would be
hidden from any direct line of site from the home. The dock would be
appropriate in appearance to the other structures on the property. The dock
can be used by property owners as well. With the easement we agree to
maintain the acre and keep is free of any dead trees and overgrowth but
will maintain privacy for the buyers. This means we will not build any
additional structures (except for the dock) or roads or anything to affect
the overall all appearance of the wooded area.

We agree to leave all major kitchen appliances, both washer and dryers,
lighting fixtures, window treatments, and we agree to pay our portion of
remaining property taxes as per the usual. We agree to pay for title search
and insurance.

Sincerely,
Derrick and Sarah Hasenour

--

## gmail:0008 | 2026-07-04 07:52 EDT
From: Darcy Knott <darcy@truebloodre.com>
To: Regina Gray <graylregina@gmail.com>, Sam <samgpresnal@gmail.com>
Subject: Attorney answer :)

Good morning

So, the short answer is yes and no 😬 You can hire an attorney for the
closing and to review everything.
Most people don't, as it's just an added expense. In Indiana we use Title
Companies to handle all the closing work (they have lawyers) so for the
most part people don't hire attorneys.
IF we were to split the land from 20 to 13 acres...yes, I would then say an
attorney is needed.
I would definitely have someone look over the easement for the lake/dock
when we receive it before closing.
For the rest of the transaction, however, an attorney is not needed.
BUT you are more than welcome to hire/use one if you would like!

I emailed another agent who is also a Real Estate Attorney but doesn't sell
often, to see what he says. However, since it's the 4th, I may not get an
answer today.

Let me know if you have more questions. We can hop on a call as well.

Thanks
Darcy

## gmail:0034 | 2026-07-07 15:36 EDT
From: Garrett Lawton <glawton@mcneelylaw.com>
To: Sam <samgpresnal@gmail.com>
Cc: Regina Gray <graylregina@gmail.com>, "darcy@truebloodre.com" <darcy@truebloodre.com>
Subject: RE: Purchase agreement and easement
Attachment files: [image001.png](property_emails_compact_attachments/email_0034_part_1_2_image001.png)

Here are some thoughts, and we can discuss more in a call. I’m available until around 5pm today and most of the day tomorrow. Just let me know on that.

Also, happy to answer whatever questions these thoughts prompt. And please don’t take any of this to mean that I don’t think you should purchase this property. It looks great, but I’m just informing you of things to be aware of heading into this transaction to hopefully ease any points of frustration or confusion later.

  *   Covenants -You’re effectively living under a traditional HOA with all the things that entails. Rather than flag each thing individually, I’d strongly encourage you to read the covenants so that you’re fully aware of each requirement, restriction, etc. Some of the most pressing in my mind are:
     *   How is the board elected and how are ties in decisions broken, given that it seems likely the board will just have two people? The covenants explicitly state that this process is outlined in the bylaws, but apparently there aren’t any bylaws.
     *   With architectural review, essentially anything you’ll want to do to the house or lot will require approval. This includes changes to the home itself (painting, additions, etc.).
        *   I would ask for approval documents on anything build after 2009, when the covenants went into effect.
        *   I would also ask for a letter from the HOA stating that the property is in good standing and there are no outstanding violations or unpaid assessments.
  *   Purchase agreement/addendum
     *   Paragraph H(1), I would change that $0 amount to match what is on the post-closing amendment.
     *   You’ll want that easement agreement asap. You’d hate to get all the way to the week of closing only to find out that there’s something in the easement agreement that they won’t budge on. But if the agreement is to benefit the neighbor, they should be flexible on this.
     *   Nothing to note on the addendum.
  *   Easement
     *   Darcy’s recommended surveyor said that he can work up the easement based on the plat. When I spoke with you earlier, I wasn’t aware that a plat existed, so I trust him on this. That said, I’d like to review that agreement once it is created.
     *   Things to consider in the easement agreement:
        *   What type of access exactly is being granted? This language should be specific.
        *   Who is responsible for maintenance to things like docks, roads, paths, etc.?
        *   Can the easement be revoked/changed, and if so, how?
        *   Is this easement going to the property owners for the duration of their lives or running with their property? Assuming this is for the benefit of the neighbors.
        *   Insurance/liability considerations.
        *   Potential limitation on users/guests.

From: Sam <samgpresnal@gmail.com>

To: Garrett Lawton <glawton@mcneelylaw.com>
Cc: Regina Gray <graylregina@gmail.com>; darcy@truebloodre.com
Subject: Re: Purchase agreement and easement

On Tue, Jul 7, 2026 at 4:25 PM Sam <samgpresnal@gmail.com<mailto:samgpresnal@gmail.com>> wrote:
Thanks for sharing your thoughts. We'd like to discuss this today if possible. If you're still available call me @ 574-850-8256.

On Tue, Jul 7, 2026 at 3:36 PM Garrett Lawton <glawton@mcneelylaw.com<mailto:glawton@mcneelylaw.com>> wrote:
Here are some thoughts, and we can discuss more in a call. I’m available until around 5pm today and most of the day tomorrow. Just let me know on that.

Also, happy to answer whatever questions these thoughts prompt. And please don’t take any of this to mean that I don’t think you should purchase this property. It looks great, but I’m just informing you of things to be aware of heading into this transaction to hopefully ease any points of frustration or confusion later.

  *   Covenants -You’re effectively living under a traditional HOA with all the things that entails. Rather than flag each thing individually, I’d strongly encourage you to read the covenants so that you’re fully aware of each requirement, restriction, etc. Some of the most pressing in my mind are:

     *   How is the board elected and how are ties in decisions broken, given that it seems likely the board will just have two people? The covenants explicitly state that this process is outlined in the bylaws, but apparently there aren’t any bylaws.
     *   With architectural review, essentially anything you’ll want to do to the house or lot will require approval. This includes changes to the home itself (painting, additions, etc.).

        *   I would ask for approval documents on anything build after 2009, when the covenants went into effect.
        *   I would also ask for a letter from the HOA stating that the property is in good standing and there are no outstanding violations or unpaid assessments.

  *   Purchase agreement/addendum

     *   Paragraph H(1), I would change that $0 amount to match what is on the post-closing amendment.
     *   You’ll want that easement agreement asap. You’d hate to get all the way to the week of closing only to find out that there’s something in the easement agreement that they won’t budge on. But if the agreement is to benefit the neighbor, they should be flexible on this.
     *   Nothing to note on the addendum.

  *   Easement

     *   Darcy’s recommended surveyor said that he can work up the easement based on the plat. When I spoke with you earlier, I wasn’t aware that a plat existed, so I trust him on this. That said, I’d like to review that agreement once it is created.
     *   Things to consider in the easement agreement:

        *   What type of access exactly is being granted? This language should be specific.
        *   Who is responsible for maintenance to things like docks, roads, paths, etc.?
        *   Can the easement be revoked/changed, and if so, how?
        *   Is this easement going to the property owners for the duration of their lives or running with their property? Assuming this is for the benefit of the neighbors.
        *   Insurance/liability considerations.
        *   Potential limitation on users/guests.

## gmail:0047 | 2026-07-09 12:58 EDT
From: Garrett Lawton <glawton@mcneelylaw.com>
To: Sam <samgpresnal@gmail.com>
Cc: Darcy Knott <darcy@truebloodre.com>, Regina Gray <graylregina@gmail.com>
Subject: Re: Owner counter offer review

Good afternoon,

I would not advise agreeing to an easement before you’ve seen it. If it simply cannot be prepared prior to closing, I would advise not making the sale contingent on the easement, and then you could just grant one after you’ve purchased the property. Obviously, this means you’re not obligated to grant an easement at all, but I think that’s a fair trade for the sellers to make if they can’t produce an easement document prior to close.

Just FYI, I’m headed out of town for a few days. I will try to periodically check my emails but if you have anything pressing, just have Darcy shoot me a text and I will take a look. I’ll have time here and there to assist.

From: Sam <samgpresnal@gmail.com>
Sent: Wednesday, 08 July 2026 16:55:11
To: Garrett Lawton <glawton@mcneelylaw.com>
Cc: Darcy Knott <darcy@truebloodre.com>; Regina Gray <graylregina@gmail.com>
Subject: Owner counter offer review

HI Garrett,

Attached is the sellers' counteroffer (on pages 10 & 11). Note that they mentioned 2 acres for the easement, but they admitted this was a mistake and the correct amount is 1 acre.

We are most concerned with Section 4, which states the easement may not be available for review prior to closing; we are not comfortable with this. How do you suggest we word a response to this?  We want the easement documents available for review at least two weeks prior to closing.

Thanks!
Sam and Regina

easement with access to the lake depicted by the exhibits, or something to
that effect? Obviously, the written portion done by a lawyer has to be
finalized with our signatures, thus we both have to agree to the wording no
matter what. All we want in the purchase agreement is that they agree to
allow us access to the lake for fishing, adding a dock in the location we
already showed them and in return we keep the 1-acre wooded area
maintained. If something other than that is written in the easement, they
can refuse to sign it, and it will not go into effect. There is no real way
of "pulling a fast one" if that makes sense? So, from what I can tell as
long as we put something in the purchase agreement stating the buyers agree
to grant the sellers a recreational easement as shown in exhibit "A" prior
to or after closing when both parties are in agreeance to the terms, should
work. That would protect both parties and give them time to review with a
council before signing anything.

Thanks,
Derrick

--

## gmail:0131 | 2026-08-02 11:45 EDT
From: Darcy Knott <darcy@truebloodre.com>
To: Sam <samgpresnal@gmail.com>, Regina Gray <graylregina@gmail.com>
Subject: Fwd: [EXTERNAL] Insurance Quote - 5306 State Road 144, Greenwood, IN 46143

Explanation of claims

---------- Forwarded message ---------
From: Derrick Hasenour <derrickhasenour@gmail.com>
Date: Sat, Aug 1, 2026 at 5:41 PM
Subject: Re: [EXTERNAL] Insurance Quote - 5306 State Road 144, Greenwood,
IN 46143
To: Darcy Knott <darcy@truebloodre.com>

Good evening,

Sorry, with all the emails I'm getting in relation to the home and other
personal issues this one got lost in the fold. These claims have an
interesting back story that ended up causing us to switch insurance
companies.

Claims

   1. The 25K claim was because of a few bats that found their way into our
   attic. The claim paid to have all attic entry points to be sealed thus
   preventing any further "bat infestations". Bats are a protected and they
   require special care to remove them and prevent further entry. In addition
   to the work needed to seal the attic, the money was used to also remove any
   and all bat waste/insulation, disinfect and re-insulate areas affected in
   the attic. This was done in 9/2024, and we have had no further issues.
   2. One of the water claims was actually not a claim at all, but one was
   due to the fact I called after hours with a question which triggered a
   claim to be created. Apparently if you call with questions during the day,
   you are forwarded to your agency where the questions are answered in an
   appropriate manner. If you happen to call the general number after work
   hours as soon as the phone is picked up, a claim is created automatically.
   Obviously, I had no idea this was the process as I have never had a claim
   prior. The reason I called back in 2021 is because we found a dried water
   stain below the master bathroom. We had lived in the home for about a year
   prior to the stain being noticed. It was not wet but I called to see if I
   needed to notify my insurance company of what we found so it could be

Good morning Leslie,

The sellers got back to me. Below is there response to the claims.

--------

Claims:

1. The 25K claim was because of a few bats that found their way into our
attic. The claim paid to have all attic entry points to be sealed thus
preventing any further "bat infestations". Bats are a protected and they
require special care to remove them and prevent further entry. In addition
to the work needed to seal the attic, the money was used to also remove any
and all bat waste/insulation, disinfect and re-insulate areas affected in
the attic. This was done in 9/2024, and we have had no further issues.

2. One of the water claims was actually not a claim at all, but one was due
to the fact I called after hours with a question which triggered a claim to
be created. Apparently if you call with questions during the day, you are
forwarded to your agency where the questions are answered in an appropriate
manner. If you happen to call the general number after work hours as soon
as the phone is picked up, a claim is created automatically. Obviously, I
had no idea this was the process as I have never had a claim prior. The
reason I called back in 2021 is because we found a dried water stain below
the master bathroom. We had lived in the home for about a year prior to the
stain being noticed. It was not wet but I called to see if I needed to
notify my insurance company of what we found so it could be documented for
the future. This led to the "water claim" being filed. The next morning the
claims adjusted called, and I realized a claim had actually been created
without them telling me. I told the claims adjuster the same info I did to
the person the night before, and the claim was closed. We later found out
from the prior owners one of the children overflowed the shower one time
leading to the staining and no further water issues have been noticed
since. Just in case we professionally sealed the base to ensure there would
be no future risks.

3. The 2nd "water clam" in 2023 was triggered when a contracting company
was hired to remove a retaining wall from the back yard, redirecting water
drainage away from the downstairs patio and install a new retaining wall
for a future pool. My insurance company suggested and strongly encouraged I
create a claim in case something developed in relation to water damage to
patio area so it could be documented appropriately by them and us. This
documentation would be used in a court situation if needed. Luckly, we
ended up getting a new contractor who was able to redirect water away in an
appropriate manner, and we have had no further drainage concerns since. In
the end, I requested the claim being closed, and a claims adjuster was
never dispatched, and no payouts were received as noted.

From: Sam <samgpresnal@gmail.com>
Sent: Sunday, August 2, 2026 11:51 AM
To: Leslie Appel Maher <LAppel@gregoryappel.com>
Cc: Jessica Eye <JEye@gregoryappel.com>
Subject: Re: [EXTERNAL] Insurance Quote - 5306 State Road 144, Greenwood, IN 46143

Good morning Leslie,

The sellers got back to me. Below is there response to the claims.

--------

Claims:

1. The 25K claim was because of a few bats that found their way into our attic. The claim paid to have all attic entry points to be sealed thus preventing any further "bat infestations". Bats are a protected and they require special care to remove them and prevent further entry. In addition to the work needed to seal the attic, the money was used to also remove any and all bat waste/insulation, disinfect and re-insulate areas affected in the attic. This was done in 9/2024, and we have had no further issues.

2. One of the water claims was actually not a claim at all, but one was due to the fact I called after hours with a question which triggered a claim to be created. Apparently if you call with questions during the day, you are forwarded to your agency where the questions are answered in an appropriate manner. If you happen to call the general number after work hours as soon as the phone is picked up, a claim is created automatically. Obviously, I had no idea this was the process as I have never had a claim prior. The reason I called back in 2021 is because we found a dried water stain below the master bathroom. We had lived in the home for about a year prior to the stain being noticed. It was not wet but I called to see if I needed to notify my insurance company of what we found so it could be documented for the future. This led to the "water claim" being filed. The next morning the claims adjusted called, and I realized a claim had actually been created without them telling me. I told the claims adjuster the same info I did to the person the night before, and the claim was closed. We later found out from the prior owners one of the children overflowed the shower one time leading to the staining and no further water issues have been noticed since. Just in case we professionally sealed the base to ensure there would be no future risks.

3. The 2nd "water clam" in 2023 was triggered when a contracting company was hired to remove a retaining wall from the back yard, redirecting water drainage away from the downstairs patio and install a new retaining wall for a future pool. My insurance company suggested and strongly encouraged I create a claim in case something developed in relation to water damage to patio area so it could be documented appropriately by them and us. This documentation would be used in a court situation if needed. Luckly, we ended up getting a new contractor who was able to redirect water away in an appropriate manner, and we have had no further drainage concerns since. In the end, I requested the claim being closed, and a claims adjuster was never dispatched, and no payouts were received as noted.

## gmail:0164 | 2026-08-05 12:23 EDT
From: Regina Gray <graylregina@gmail.com>
To: Darcy Knott <darcy@truebloodre.com>, Sam <samgpresnal@gmail.com>
Subject: Adjusted Offers:

*Hi Darcy,*
*Below our three options we wish to provide to the seller. We only have one
question regarding option 1. Is it ok to keep as is and ask for the 30k off
even though current inspection estimates are adding up to 24k or should we
make it 25k off? Other than that we are good to go with the following. *

At this time, we are not comfortable proceeding with the purchase of the
home at the original purchase price of *$2,500,000* while accepting the
easement and before all inspection results and repair estimates have been
received and reviewed.

Based on the information currently available, we would like to present the
following two options for the sellers’ consideration:
Option 1:

   - *Purchase Price:* *$2,470,000*
   - *Easement:* Remains in place
   - *Closing Date:* *August 12*
   - *Inspection Contingency:* Subject to our review of the pending mold
   test results and a complete HVAC inspection and repair estimate

The revised purchase price reflects approximately *$24,224.37* in estimated
repairs related to the plumbing, roof, septic system, and electrical system.

We have not yet received a complete HVAC inspection or an estimate for the
repairs identified during the home inspection. Simply stating that the HVAC
systems are currently operating properly is not an adequate inspection
result. We are requesting a professional evaluation of the issues
identified during the home inspection, along with written estimates for any
recommended repairs or replacement.

We are not comfortable proceeding at the original purchase price without
fully understanding the condition of the HVAC systems and the potential
costs involved, as HVAC repairs or replacement could exceed *$30,000*.

We also expect to receive the mold inspection results tomorrow. Moving the
closing date to *August 12* will provide adequate time to receive and
review the mold results and obtain the requested HVAC evaluation and repair
estimates before closing.

We reserve the right to revisit and renegotiate the purchase price if
either of the following occurs:

   - The mold test is positive and the estimated remediation is required
   - The estimated cost of HVAC repairs or replacement exceeds *$5,000*.

Under this option, we would accept the easement and proceed at a purchase
price of *$2,470,000*, subject to the inspection conditions stated above.
Option 2:

   - *Purchase Price:* *$2,525,000*
   - *Easement:* Removed
   - *Property Condition:* Purchased as-is, with no further requests for
   repairs or credits
   - *Closing Date:* *Friday*

Under this option, we would purchase the property as-is for *$2,525,000*,
waive any further requests for repairs or credits, and close on Friday,
provided the easement is removed.

Option 3:Purchase price of *$2,450,000*, with the easement. We would not
request any additional inspections, would purchase the home as-is, and
would close on *Friday*

From: Sam <samgpresnal@gmail.com<mailto:samgpresnal@gmail.com>>
Sent: Sunday, August 2, 2026 11:51 AM
To: Leslie Appel Maher <LAppel@gregoryappel.com<mailto:LAppel@gregoryappel.com>>
Cc: Jessica Eye <JEye@gregoryappel.com<mailto:JEye@gregoryappel.com>>
Subject: Re: [EXTERNAL] Insurance Quote - 5306 State Road 144, Greenwood, IN 46143

Good morning Leslie,

The sellers got back to me. Below is there response to the claims.

--------

Claims:

1. The 25K claim was because of a few bats that found their way into our attic. The claim paid to have all attic entry points to be sealed thus preventing any further "bat infestations". Bats are a protected and they require special care to remove them and prevent further entry. In addition to the work needed to seal the attic, the money was used to also remove any and all bat waste/insulation, disinfect and re-insulate areas affected in the attic. This was done in 9/2024, and we have had no further issues.

2. One of the water claims was actually not a claim at all, but one was due to the fact I called after hours with a question which triggered a claim to be created. Apparently if you call with questions during the day, you are forwarded to your agency where the questions are answered in an appropriate manner. If you happen to call the general number after work hours as soon as the phone is picked up, a claim is created automatically. Obviously, I had no idea this was the process as I have never had a claim prior. The reason I called back in 2021 is because we found a dried water stain below the master bathroom. We had lived in the home for about a year prior to the stain being noticed. It was not wet but I called to see if I needed to notify my insurance company of what we found so it could be documented for the future. This led to the "water claim" being filed. The next morning the claims adjusted called, and I realized a claim had actually been created without them telling me. I told the claims adjuster the same info I did to the person the night before, and the claim was closed. We later found out from the prior owners one of the children overflowed the shower one time leading to the staining and no further water issues have been noticed since. Just in case we professionally sealed the base to ensure there would be no future risks.

3. The 2nd "water clam" in 2023 was triggered when a contracting company was hired to remove a retaining wall from the back yard, redirecting water drainage away from the downstairs patio and install a new retaining wall for a future pool. My insurance company suggested and strongly encouraged I create a claim in case something developed in relation to water damage to patio area so it could be documented appropriately by them and us. This documentation would be used in a court situation if needed. Luckly, we ended up getting a new contractor who was able to redirect water away in an appropriate manner, and we have had no further drainage concerns since. In the end, I requested the claim being closed, and a claims adjuster was never dispatched, and no payouts were received as noted.

Otherwise, Chubb will be the best option for home only and no additional information needed on the losses required on their end.

From: Sam <samgpresnal@gmail.com<mailto:samgpresnal@gmail.com>>
Sent: Sunday, August 2, 2026 11:51 AM
To: Leslie Appel Maher <LAppel@gregoryappel.com<mailto:LAppel@gregoryappel.com>>
Cc: Jessica Eye <JEye@gregoryappel.com<mailto:JEye@gregoryappel.com>>
Subject: Re: [EXTERNAL] Insurance Quote - 5306 State Road 144, Greenwood, IN 46143<https://www.google.com/maps/search/5306+State+Road+144,+Greenwood,+IN+46143?entry=gmail&source=g>

Good morning Leslie,

The sellers got back to me. Below is there response to the claims.

--------

Claims:

1. The 25K claim was because of a few bats that found their way into our attic. The claim paid to have all attic entry points to be sealed thus preventing any further "bat infestations". Bats are a protected and they require special care to remove them and prevent further entry. In addition to the work needed to seal the attic, the money was used to also remove any and all bat waste/insulation, disinfect and re-insulate areas affected in the attic. This was done in 9/2024, and we have had no further issues.

2. One of the water claims was actually not a claim at all, but one was due to the fact I called after hours with a question which triggered a claim to be created. Apparently if you call with questions during the day, you are forwarded to your agency where the questions are answered in an appropriate manner. If you happen to call the general number after work hours as soon as the phone is picked up, a claim is created automatically. Obviously, I had no idea this was the process as I have never had a claim prior. The reason I called back in 2021 is because we found a dried water stain below the master bathroom. We had lived in the home for about a year prior to the stain being noticed. It was not wet but I called to see if I needed to notify my insurance company of what we found so it could be documented for the future. This led to the "water claim" being filed. The next morning the claims adjusted called, and I realized a claim had actually been created without them telling me. I told the claims adjuster the same info I did to the person the night before, and the claim was closed. We later found out from the prior owners one of the children overflowed the shower one time leading to the staining and no further water issues have been noticed since. Just in case we professionally sealed the base to ensure there would be no future risks.

3. The 2nd "water clam" in 2023 was triggered when a contracting company was hired to remove a retaining wall from the back yard, redirecting water drainage away from the downstairs patio and install a new retaining wall for a future pool. My insurance company suggested and strongly encouraged I create a claim in case something developed in relation to water damage to patio area so it could be documented appropriately by them and us. This documentation would be used in a court situation if needed. Luckly, we ended up getting a new contractor who was able to redirect water away in an appropriate manner, and we have had no further drainage concerns since. In the end, I requested the claim being closed, and a claims adjuster was never dispatched, and no payouts were received as noted.

Otherwise, Chubb will be the best option for home only and no additional information needed on the losses required on their end.

> and all bat waste/insulation, disinfect and re-insulate areas affected in
> the attic. This was done in 9/2024, and we have had no further issues.

> 2. One of the water claims was actually not a claim at all, but one was
> due to the fact I called after hours with a question which triggered a
> claim to be created. Apparently if you call with questions during the day,
> you are forwarded to your agency where the questions are answered in an
> appropriate manner. If you happen to call the general number after work
> hours as soon as the phone is picked up, a claim is created automatically.
> Obviously, I had no idea this was the process as I have never had a claim
> prior. The reason I called back in 2021 is because we found a dried water
> stain below the master bathroom. We had lived in the home for about a year
> prior to the stain being noticed. It was not wet but I called to see if I
> needed to notify my insurance company of what we found so it could be
> documented for the future. This led to the "water claim" being filed. The
> next morning the claims adjusted called, and I realized a claim had
> actually been created without them telling me. I told the claims adjuster
> the same info I did to the person the night before, and the claim was
> closed. We later found out from the prior owners one of the children
> overflowed the shower one time leading to the staining and no further water
> issues have been noticed since. Just in case we professionally sealed the
> base to ensure there would be no future risks.

> 3. The 2nd "water clam" in 2023 was triggered when a contracting company
> was hired to remove a retaining wall from the back yard, redirecting water
> drainage away from the downstairs patio and install a new retaining wall
> for a future pool. My insurance company suggested and strongly encouraged I
> create a claim in case something developed in relation to water damage to
> patio area so it could be documented appropriately by them and us. This
> documentation would be used in a court situation if needed. Luckly, we
> ended up getting a new contractor who was able to redirect water away in an
> appropriate manner, and we have had no further drainage concerns since. In
> the end, I requested the claim being closed, and a claims adjuster was
> never dispatched, and no payouts were received as noted.

Good morning Leslie,

The sellers got back to me. Below is there response to the claims.

--------

Claims:

1. The 25K claim was because of a few bats that found their way into our attic. The claim paid to have all attic entry points to be sealed thus preventing any further "bat infestations". Bats are a protected and they require special care to remove them and prevent further entry. In addition to the work needed to seal the attic, the money was used to also remove any and all bat waste/insulation, disinfect and re-insulate areas affected in the attic. This was done in 9/2024, and we have had no further issues.

2. One of the water claims was actually not a claim at all, but one was due to the fact I called after hours with a question which triggered a claim to be created. Apparently if you call with questions during the day, you are forwarded to your agency where the questions are answered in an appropriate manner. If you happen to call the general number after work hours as soon as the phone is picked up, a claim is created automatically. Obviously, I had no idea this was the process as I have never had a claim prior. The reason I called back in 2021 is because we found a dried water stain below the master bathroom. We had lived in the home for about a year prior to the stain being noticed. It was not wet but I called to see if I needed to notify my insurance company of what we found so it could be documented for the future. This led to the "water claim" being filed. The next morning the claims adjusted called, and I realized a claim had actually been created without them telling me. I told the claims adjuster the same info I did to the person the night before, and the claim was closed. We later found out from the prior owners one of the children overflowed the shower one time leading to the staining and no further water issues have been noticed since. Just in case we professionally sealed the base to ensure there would be no future risks.

3. The 2nd "water clam" in 2023 was triggered when a contracting company was hired to remove a retaining wall from the back yard, redirecting water drainage away from the downstairs patio and install a new retaining wall for a future pool. My insurance company suggested and strongly encouraged I create a claim in case something developed in relation to water damage to patio area so it could be documented appropriately by them and us. This documentation would be used in a court situation if needed. Luckly, we ended up getting a new contractor who was able to redirect water away in an appropriate manner, and we have had no further drainage concerns since. In the end, I requested the claim being closed, and a claims adjuster was never dispatched, and no payouts were received as noted.

Good morning Leslie,

The sellers got back to me. Below is there response to the claims.

--------

Claims:

1. The 25K claim was because of a few bats that found their way into our attic. The claim paid to have all attic entry points to be sealed thus preventing any further "bat infestations". Bats are a protected and they require special care to remove them and prevent further entry. In addition to the work needed to seal the attic, the money was used to also remove any and all bat waste/insulation, disinfect and re-insulate areas affected in the attic. This was done in 9/2024, and we have had no further issues.

2. One of the water claims was actually not a claim at all, but one was due to the fact I called after hours with a question which triggered a claim to be created. Apparently if you call with questions during the day, you are forwarded to your agency where the questions are answered in an appropriate manner. If you happen to call the general number after work hours as soon as the phone is picked up, a claim is created automatically. Obviously, I had no idea this was the process as I have never had a claim prior. The reason I called back in 2021 is because we found a dried water stain below the master bathroom. We had lived in the home for about a year prior to the stain being noticed. It was not wet but I called to see if I needed to notify my insurance company of what we found so it could be documented for the future. This led to the "water claim" being filed. The next morning the claims adjusted called, and I realized a claim had actually been created without them telling me. I told the claims adjuster the same info I did to the person the night before, and the claim was closed. We later found out from the prior owners one of the children overflowed the shower one time leading to the staining and no further water issues have been noticed since. Just in case we professionally sealed the base to ensure there would be no future risks.

3. The 2nd "water clam" in 2023 was triggered when a contracting company was hired to remove a retaining wall from the back yard, redirecting water drainage away from the downstairs patio and install a new retaining wall for a future pool. My insurance company suggested and strongly encouraged I create a claim in case something developed in relation to water damage to patio area so it could be documented appropriately by them and us. This documentation would be used in a court situation if needed. Luckly, we ended up getting a new contractor who was able to redirect water away in an appropriate manner, and we have had no further drainage concerns since. In the end, I requested the claim being closed, and a claims adjuster was never dispatched, and no payouts were received as noted.

> Claims:

> 1. The 25K claim was because of a few bats that found their way into our
> attic. The claim paid to have all attic entry points to be sealed thus
> preventing any further "bat infestations". Bats are a protected and they
> require special care to remove them and prevent further entry. In addition
> to the work needed to seal the attic, the money was used to also remove any
> and all bat waste/insulation, disinfect and re-insulate areas affected in
> the attic. This was done in 9/2024, and we have had no further issues.

> 2. One of the water claims was actually not a claim at all, but one was
> due to the fact I called after hours with a question which triggered a
> claim to be created. Apparently if you call with questions during the day,
> you are forwarded to your agency where the questions are answered in an
> appropriate manner. If you happen to call the general number after work
> hours as soon as the phone is picked up, a claim is created automatically.
> Obviously, I had no idea this was the process as I have never had a claim
> prior. The reason I called back in 2021 is because we found a dried water
> stain below the master bathroom. We had lived in the home for about a year
> prior to the stain being noticed. It was not wet but I called to see if I
> needed to notify my insurance company of what we found so it could be
> documented for the future. This led to the "water claim" being filed. The
> next morning the claims adjusted called, and I realized a claim had
> actually been created without them telling me. I told the claims adjuster
> the same info I did to the person the night before, and the claim was
> closed. We later found out from the prior owners one of the children
> overflowed the shower one time leading to the staining and no further water
> issues have been noticed since. Just in case we professionally sealed the
> base to ensure there would be no future risks.

> 3. The 2nd "water clam" in 2023 was triggered when a contracting company
> was hired to remove a retaining wall from the back yard, redirecting water
> drainage away from the downstairs patio and install a new retaining wall
> for a future pool. My insurance company suggested and strongly encouraged I
> create a claim in case something developed in relation to water damage to
> patio area so it could be documented appropriately by them and us. This
> documentation would be used in a court situation if needed. Luckly, we
> ended up getting a new contractor who was able to redirect water away in an
> appropriate manner, and we have had no further drainage concerns since. In
> the end, I requested the claim being closed, and a claims adjuster was
> never dispatched, and no payouts were received as noted.

Subject: Utilities and other information

Attachment files: [IMG_20260807_172325 (1).jpg](property_emails_compact_attachments/email_0214_part_1_2_IMG_20260807_172325%20_1_.jpg) · [IMG_20260807_172325.jpg](property_emails_compact_attachments/email_0214_part_1_3_IMG_20260807_172325.jpg) · [IMG_20260807_172336 (1).jpg](property_emails_compact_attachments/email_0214_part_1_4_IMG_20260807_172336%20_1_.jpg) · [IMG_20260807_172336 (2).jpg](property_emails_compact_attachments/email_0214_part_1_5_IMG_20260807_172336%20_2_.jpg) · [IMG_20260807_172335.jpg](property_emails_compact_attachments/email_0214_part_1_6_IMG_20260807_172335.jpg)

Good afternoon,

I am glad we can finally communicate without "The Realtor" being stuck in the middle. I do not feel her presents was at all helpful in the transaction making it much more complicated that it really needed to be. If she had just provided us with your contact information, we could have bypassed a lot of miscommunications.

Utilities List @ 5306 State Rd 144 and 5306 ½ State Rd 144 Greenwood, IN 46143
1.  Electric — Johnson County REMC (JCREMC)
Email: custservice@jcremc.com<mailto:custservice@jcremc.com>
Phone: 317.736.6174
Hours: Monday-Friday 7:30 a.m. to 4:30 p.m.
Address:
750 International Dr.
P.O. Box 309
Franklin, IN 46131
2.  Water — Bargersville Utilities
[long link omitted]
3.  Fiber Internet — Johnson County Fiber (JCFiber)
Residential Services
317-736-6174
  Info@jcfiber.com<mailto:fiber@jcremc.com?subject=JCFiber%20Residential%20Services>
4.  Trash and Recycling — Best Way Disposal
800-354-1830
Fax 317-270-5548
2577 Kentucky Avenue
Indianapolis, IN 46221
5. Natural Gas — CenterPoint Energy
https://www.citizensenergygroup.com/My-Account/Start-Stop-Transfer.aspx?Type=Start
6.  Entry Gate Maintenance — K&K Fencing
Address: 6520 Brookville Road Indianapolis, IN 46219
Phone: 317-463-5473
7. Cellular Service Gate Entry — DoorKing Inc
Access Control Solutions since 1948
120 Glasgow Avenue, Inglewood, California 90301 U.S.A.
Tel: 310-645-0023   FAX: 310-641-1586

We will leave all the information we have in relation to drawings of the home, lake, landscaping, and property in the laundry room when you come on 8/21/26. We will also leave keys and codes to the doors, front door, keycodes, garage doors, gate, and barn. The gate phone number to open and close is 317-443-5921. Call 317-443-5921 and wait for the beep then use 4 to keep open and 3 to close. The gate code we will give you upon arrival.

General Maintenance of Gate/common area
The right side of the gate is yours to care for. I usually need to pull/spray weeds at least once a month. The grasses will need to be cut now and again once winter sets in. The gate openers need the gear boxes check for oil needs at least yearly, and all gate hinges need greased at least 2 times a year. The gate opener has a cellular connection and has a monthly service contract which will be billed thru DoorKing as above. The lights up the lane are on a timer which is hidden in the grass up at the edge of your property and the neighbors along with a large breaker box that provides power to the lights and gate. The timer is located just below, and it will need to be adjusted with season changes and daylight savings time changes.
Lake
The lake will need to have its usual algae treatment completed for the whole lake and then spot treated as appropriate. The lake will need dye added as well to reduce undergrowth late in the season. Please also make sure to watch out for and treat duck weed as needed because it quadruples in amount every 48hrs. The best treatment is fluoride based and costs around $200 an ounce or so but works very very well. The fountain will need to be removed before the first freeze and cleaned. The pump must be stored in a water/antifreeze solution in a place where it won't freeze. The circuit can then be shut off at the breaker box.
Barn:
Make sure to plug up the heat tapes as soon as the freezing weather sets in or pipes will freeze in the barn and bathroom. The key code are the same as the home. You will want to purchase 2 dehumidifiers to keep running all the time to reduce moister and condensation. This keeps the fans from dropping and rust reduction. Make sure to always treat mice or consider a cat. Raccoons also like to dig their way under the back side of the barn so might need traps or just monitor closely to keep them out.

Furniture you asked about along with pricing.

  1.
Beach Manufacturing Pool table w/ sticks and caddy and rug plus bar stools in downstairs kitchen area for free along with purchase. — $2500
  2.
3 Outdoor couches and 2 glass top side tables -- $300
  3.
4 Loungers with cushions and 2 glass top side tables -- $300

If you have any further questions please feel free to ask.

Derrick and Sarah

Derrick 317-292-1883
Sarah 317-292-1884

>>> The lake will need to have its usual algae treatment completed for the

>>> added as well to reduce undergrowth late in the season. Please also make
>>> sure to watch out for and treat duck weed as needed because it quadruples
>>> in amount every 48hrs. The best treatment is fluoride based and costs
>>> around $200 an ounce or so but works very very well. The fountain will need
>>> to be removed before the first freeze and cleaned. The pump must be stored
>>> in a water/antifreeze solution in a place where it won't freeze. The
>>> circuit can then be shut off at the breaker box.

>>> in or pipes will freeze in the barn and bathroom. The key code are the same

>>> the time to reduce moister and condensation. This keeps the fans from
>>> dropping and rust reduction. Make sure to always treat mice or consider a
>>> cat. Raccoons also like to dig their way under the back side of the barn so
>>> might need traps or just monitor closely to keep them out.

Subject: Re: [EXTERNAL] Insurance Quote - 5306 State Road 144, Greenwood, IN 46143<https://www.google.com/maps/search/5306+State+Road+144,+Greenwood,+IN+46143?entry=gmail&source=g>

Good morning Leslie,

The sellers got back to me. Below is there response to the claims.

--------

Claims:

1. The 25K claim was because of a few bats that found their way into our attic. The claim paid to have all attic entry points to be sealed thus preventing any further "bat infestations". Bats are a protected and they require special care to remove them and prevent further entry. In addition to the work needed to seal the attic, the money was used to also remove any and all bat waste/insulation, disinfect and re-insulate areas affected in the attic. This was done in 9/2024, and we have had no further issues.

2. One of the water claims was actually not a claim at all, but one was due to the fact I called after hours with a question which triggered a claim to be created. Apparently if you call with questions during the day, you are forwarded to your agency where the questions are answered in an appropriate manner. If you happen to call the general number after work hours as soon as the phone is picked up, a claim is created automatically. Obviously, I had no idea this was the process as I have never had a claim prior. The reason I called back in 2021 is because we found a dried water stain below the master bathroom. We had lived in the home for about a year prior to the stain being noticed. It was not wet but I called to see if I needed to notify my insurance company of what we found so it could be documented for the future. This led to the "water claim" being filed. The next morning the claims adjusted called, and I realized a claim had actually been created without them telling me. I told the claims adjuster the same info I did to the person the night before, and the claim was closed. We later found out from the prior owners one of the children overflowed the shower one time leading to the staining and no further water issues have been noticed since. Just in case we professionally sealed the base to ensure there would be no future risks.

3. The 2nd "water clam" in 2023 was triggered when a contracting company was hired to remove a retaining wall from the back yard, redirecting water drainage away from the downstairs patio and install a new retaining wall for a future pool. My insurance company suggested and strongly encouraged I create a claim in case something developed in relation to water damage to patio area so it could be documented appropriately by them and us. This documentation would be used in a court situation if needed. Luckly, we ended up getting a new contractor who was able to redirect water away in an appropriate manner, and we have had no further drainage concerns since. In the end, I requested the claim being closed, and a claims adjuster was never dispatched, and no payouts were received as noted.

here.

Good morning!
Just sending further information from the other realtor:

Hi Darcy
Yes , all neighbors knew/ know of this document and we are not pending.
Rachel Freitas and I have Dr. Norris's home for sale at 5352 St Rd 144.
When I had preliminary Title Work done the Attorney at the Title Company said that it is necessary to further define
the relationship ( between the 3 owners ) that utilize the gate and shared drive at the entry.
It is my understanding that each homeowner has been putting in approx $1000 a year to take care of the entry/gate etc..
I believe that this neighborly arrangement was by handshake.

I have used Transantion Title ( for decades ) and the Attorney Tim Warner recommended this.
He found that the HOA did not sign off on the original Grant of Easement so he created this amendment.
This will benefit all 3 owners.
I completely understand that they will want their attorney to review.

Norris and Fletcher have agreed to sign.

Hopefully we can move this forward as soon as possible.

Garrett, please just let us all know what you think. Obviously an agreement was already there but this sounds/looks like they just want it tightened up a bit!

Thanks
Darcy

can communicate
and come to a resolution ( hopefully ) that everyone will be ok with.
We have heard Hassenhours thoughts that damage was done to the road during
the construction of John's house, approx 2017.
I believe that 5306 was built in 2001, that makes the main portion of the
drive 25 years old. I have zero clue if the road was ever improved/
sealcoated etc.. in that time period.
Fletcher only uses a small part of the drive, and John uses the part up to
his entry ( and the portion that crosses land owned by Sam and Regina).

and John has shared that Hassenhour was " running a business " out of the
barn in the back.
I guess you all need to have a discussion as to where you believe this
issue is, ( or if there is actually an issue)and can it be dealt with
easily.
I dont want to be an arbitrator in this - but I'll try. ( I am not an
attorney.)

Regina and Sam What is the issue , what would you propose needs to be
remedied and how ?

I dont know what happened between John and The Hassenhours to challenge
their relationship ?
But hopefully we can " get your neighborly relationship started off on the
right foot ".
And of course, John hopes to sell his place soon ( which we are working on
).
I will use the word " hopeful " again, hopefully we will have a new
neighbor to introduce you to soon.

I will also send you all the docs on these properties that have been
recorded in Johnson County, ( at least the ones I have been given by John ).

Dean Hicks

are ready to move forward with revised amendment )

## gmail:0272 | 2026-09-05 13:01 EDT
From: Luke Fletcher <lukemfletcher@gmail.com>
To: Dean Hicks <deanhicks53@gmail.com>
Cc: Regina Gray <graylregina@gmail.com>, laura.leverton@hotmail.com, John Norris OBGYN <jnorris@johnsonmemorial.org>, Rachel Freitas <rachel@rachelfreitashomes.com>, Sam <samgpresnal@gmail.com>
Subject: Re: Wellington Minor Subdivision Hasenour/Fletcher/Norris 5306 St Rd 144 Greenwood

Hi Dean
Thanks for reaching out. A lot of the history on this pre-dates us, but
here is what we were able to piece together:

When John had his built, the trucks damaged the common road. There was a
verbal agreement made between John and the McClains (previous owners of
5306) that he would pay for the repairs. Time passed and the Hasenhour’s
purchased 5306, and the agreement was brought back up for John to cover
repairs. Apparently both John and the Hasenhours got separate quotes for
the repairs, which estimated around $30k.

More time passed without anything being done. So we felt it made sense to
get the common road back to square one before signing a new easement
agreement.

Again, this is a lot of second hand and third hand information but that is
the story to the best of our knowledge.

Thanks
Luke

From: Sam <samgpresnal@gmail.com>
To: Amber Knapp <aknapp@wbwlawyers.com>
Subject: Fwd: [EXTERNAL] Insurance Quote - 5306 State Road 144, Greenwood, IN 46143

---------- Forwarded message ---------
From: Darcy Knott <darcy@truebloodre.com>
Date: Sun, Aug 2, 2026, 11:45 AM
Subject: Fwd: [EXTERNAL] Insurance Quote - 5306 State Road 144, Greenwood,
IN 46143
To: Sam <samgpresnal@gmail.com>, Regina Gray <graylregina@gmail.com>

Explanation of claims

---------- Forwarded message ---------
From: Derrick Hasenour <derrickhasenour@gmail.com>
Date: Sat, Aug 1, 2026 at 5:41 PM
Subject: Re: [EXTERNAL] Insurance Quote - 5306 State Road 144, Greenwood,
IN 46143

To: Darcy Knott <darcy@truebloodre.com>

Good evening,

Sorry, with all the emails I'm getting in relation to the home and other
personal issues this one got lost in the fold. These claims have an
interesting back story that ended up causing us to switch insurance
companies.

Claims

   1. The 25K claim was because of a few bats that found their way into our
   attic. The claim paid to have all attic entry points to be sealed thus
   preventing any further "bat infestations". Bats are a protected and they
   require special care to remove them and prevent further entry. In addition
   to the work needed to seal the attic, the money was used to also remove any
   and all bat waste/insulation, disinfect and re-insulate areas affected in
   the attic. This was done in 9/2024, and we have had no further issues.
   2. One of the water claims was actually not a claim at all, but one was
   due to the fact I called after hours with a question which triggered a
   claim to be created. Apparently if you call with questions during the day,
   you are forwarded to your agency where the questions are answered in an
   appropriate manner. If you happen to call the general number after work
   hours as soon as the phone is picked up, a claim is created automatically.
   Obviously, I had no idea this was the process as I have never had a claim
   prior. The reason I called back in 2021 is because we found a dried water
   stain below the master bathroom. We had lived in the home for about a year
   prior to the stain being noticed. It was not wet but I called to see if I
   needed to notify my insurance company of what we found so it could be
   documented for the future. This led to the "water claim" being filed. The
   next morning the claims adjusted called, and I realized a claim had
   actually been created without them telling me. I told the claims adjuster
   the same info I did to the person the night before, and the claim was
   closed. We later found out from the prior owners one of the children
   overflowed the shower one time leading to the staining and no further water
   issues have been noticed since. Just in case we professionally sealed the
   base to ensure there would be no future risks.
   3. The 2nd "water clam" in 2023 was triggered when a contracting company
   was hired to remove a retaining wall from the back yard, redirecting water
   drainage away from the downstairs patio and install a new retaining wall
   for a future pool.  My insurance company suggested and strongly encouraged
   I create a claim in case something developed in relation to water damage to
   patio area so it could be documented appropriately by them and us. This
   documentation would be used in a court situation if needed. Luckly, we
   ended up getting a new contractor who was able to redirect water away in an
   appropriate manner, and we have had no further drainage concerns since. In
   the end, I requested the claim being closed, and a claims adjuster was
   never dispatched, and no payouts were received as noted.

Derrick and Sarah Hasenour
------------------------------
